### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
여전히 가격 상승 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 95.8조원, 영업이익 75.1조원으로 예상한다. 
이전 전망 대비 감소한 수준이다. 원/달러 환율 영향이다. 영업이익률은 78.4%로 전 
분기 대비 2.1%p 상승할 전망이다. DRAM 매출액은 73.1조원, 영업이익 
59.2조원으로 추정한다. DRAM ASP는 이전 전망 대비 상향 조정했다. 일부 제품 
가격 인상을 반영한 영향이다. Bit Growth는 이전 전망 수준을 유지했다. ASP 
상향이 수익성 개선에 미친 영향은 크지 않을 전망이다. NAND 매출액은 22.4조원, 
영업이익은 15.9조원으로 예상한다. 
Global Top 수준 주주환원 규모/자사주 살 게 더 있다 
SK하이닉스는 2026년 8월 19일 주주환원에 대한 새로운 정책을 제시했다. FCF에서 
CAPEX를 제외한 금액의 50% 이상을 주주환원에 사용한다는게 핵심이다. 2026년 
기준으로 최소 90조원 수준일 것으로 추정한다. 자사주 중심으로 주주환원을 계획 
중이고 이미 집행 중인 40조원 이외에 4분기 중에 추가로 자사주 매입이 진행될 
것으로 예상한다. 배당도 일부 집행이 되겠지만 자사주가 중심이 될 전망이다. 
흔들리지 말아야 할 이유는 많다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 모델의 성장으로 메모리에 
대한 수요가 지속적으로 높아지고 있고, 이에 비해 공급 증가는 제한적인 상황이 
상당기간 지속될 것으로 전망하기 때문이다. 이로 인해 현재 수준의 DRAM, NAND 
가격에 변동성은 낮고, 높은 수익성이 유지되면서 안정적 현금흐름 창출이 가능할 
것으로 전망한다. 현재 주가는 지나치게 저평가되어 있는 국면에 있다고 판단한다. 
목표주가는 12개월 예상 EPS 446,092원에 PER 9 배를 적용한 4,000,000원을 
유지한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

Document 227: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

Document 268: AI可以免疫高利率？泡沫末期，市场往往充斥着幻想

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI可以免疫高利率？泡沫末期，市场往往充斥着幻想.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

当分析师⾼喊 " ⾼利率不影响 AI 繁荣 " 时，历史的回响令⼈不安 —— 这与 2000 年互联⽹泡
沫、 2008 年⾦融危机前⼣的叙事如出⼀辙。数据已在⽆声⽰警： Alphabet 、 Meta 、微软等
科技巨头表外承诺总规模达 4.11 万亿美元， 90 天内暴增近 1 万亿；纳斯达克 100 本⽉市值蒸
发逾 1.2 万亿美元。表外承诺不随股价消失， AI 商业化收⼊能否赶在债务浪潮到来前完成追
赶？
摘要：
当债券收益率攀升，分析师们却挥挥⼿说 "AI 繁荣不受影响 "—— 这是经典的泡沫逻辑。历史上每
⼀次重⼤繁荣的终结，⽆不伴随着融资条件的收紧；⽽每⼀次泡沫末期，市场都会涌现出各种
理由，声称 " 这⼀次不同 " 。 AI 热潮能否真正免疫⾼利率，答案或许已在数字中悄然浮现。
以 Alphabet 、 Meta 、微软、亚⻢逊为核⼼的超⼤规模云计算企业及 Nvidia 、 Broadcom 、
SpaceX ，其披露的表外承诺总规模已达 4.11 万亿美元 —— 仅在约 90 天内新增承诺接近 1 万亿美

元。与此同时，这些公司账⾯上的实际债务和租赁负债合计约 1 万亿美元，意味着每 1 美元表内
负债背后，对应着超过 4 美元未在资产负债表上显⽰的承诺。与之形成对照的是，纳斯达克 100
指数成份股本⽉市值合计蒸发逾 1.2 万亿美元。
表外承诺不因股价涨跌⽽消失，到期必须兑付。当前的核⼼悬念在于：AI 商业化收⼊能否在下
⼀轮承诺浪潮到来之前实现追赶。⽽随着⾼利率对 AI ⼦⾏业的冲击逐步显现，市场正在从狂热
⾛向分化，资本配置趋于审慎。
泡沫末期的惯常叙事：⾼利率 " 不重要 "
Rockefeller International 主席 Ruchir Sharma 指出，每当债券收益率上⾏，总有分析师声称 AI 繁
荣可以独善其⾝，⽽这恰恰是经典的泡沫逻辑 —— 资⾦价格永远重要，且有充分理由相信，当
下⽐以往任何时候都更为如此。
2000 年互联⽹泡沫期间，市场流⾏的神话是 " 互联⽹产品的巨⼤需求 " 使科技公司摆脱了利率等
普通经济规律的约束。 2008 年危机前⼣，分析师则试图论证 " 利率上升不会刺破泡沫 " ，或最多

只会放缓⽽⾮逆转资产价格涨势，因为企业盈利处于 "30 年来最佳⽔平 " 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
118 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
如今相似的论调以 AI 之名卷⼟重来：⼏个百分点的利率，对于那些正在创造历史性价值增⻓的公
司⽽⾔⽆⾜轻重；美联储以区区基点计量的加息，不可能压制那些志在赢得 AI 军备竞赛的公司的
⽀出意愿。
Sharma 强调，此轮利率环境与 1990 年代的可⽐性有限。当前利率不仅处于⾼位，更是在近 25 年
历史性低利率之后短期内急速攀升 ——这⼀剧烈的机制转换本⾝⾜以造成⾦融压⼒。已有迹象
浮现：投资者要求以更⾼收益率为 AI 供应链中较弱的借款⽅融资。
表外承诺急速膨胀，单季新增逾 5700 亿美元
当前 AI 基础设施投资狂潮的规模，在财务数据中呈现出惊⼈的轮廓。
据最新季报数据，以 Alphabet 、 Meta 、微软、亚⻢逊为核⼼的超⼤规模云计算企业及 Nvidia 、

Broadcom 、 SpaceX ，其披露的表外承诺总规模已达 4.11 万亿美元，较摩根⼠丹利 8 ⽉末统计的
3.12 万亿美元增加近 9920 亿美元 —— 约 90 天内新增承诺接近 1 万亿美元。如果把摩根⼠丹利此前
统计中纳⼊的、基于新闻报道⽽⾮正式财报披露的表外承诺也计算在内，总额将达到 4.29 万亿美
元。
会计处理上的不透明，正在引发直接的市场反应。
分析机构 Needham 指出，⼀旦将表外负债纳⼊计算， Meta 的企业价值将提升 35% 。 Meta 在财报
披露后信⽤违约掉期触及历史⾼位，随即宣布最⾼ 950 亿美元的股权融资计划，其中 375 亿美元
已完成，另有约 600 亿美元的 ATM 项⽬待执⾏。 Amanda Lynam 亦指出，部分企业已明确表⽰需
要在市场趋于饱和之前提前锁定资本 —— 这⼀表述被认为是 Meta 股权融资的直接反应，且未必
是最后⼀例。
摩根⼠丹利曾于 8 ⽉警告，这些承诺在算⼒供应受约束期间具有 " 战略价值 " ，但 " 若供需在预期

Document 339: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

(%)
The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.
Figure  6-3 Outlook for Japan's Main Interest
Rates

Figure  7-1 Final Demand and Intermediate Demand
Price Indexes (Goods)
Figu

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.

Document 364: Lagarde’s comments point to a December ECB rate hike

Source PDF: all report pdf/Lagarde’s comments point to a December ECB rate hike.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Upside risks to inﬂation and downside risks to growth clearly
complicate the ECB's job. However, comments at today's press
conference suggest the ECB is set to hike again in December
Carsten Brzeski
ECB President Christine Lagarde addresses a press conference following
today's meeting
Rates on hold and Lagarde out. That’s the headline from today’s
ECB meeting. But look beyond that, and the message is more
nuanced: despite growing stagﬂationary pressures, the ECB still

looks set to hike rates again in December.
The decision to keep interest rates unchanged was not a surprise.
The main argument behind this decision is that there are currently
few knock-on eﬀects from higher headline inﬂation, while ﬁnancing
conditions, mainly bond yields, have become more restrictive.
A pause after two rate hikes this year was expected, even if macro
developments since the last meeting could have justiﬁed another
rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased again in September, and

### Page 2

most leading indicators point to continued resilience in the
eurozone economy. However, the surge in bond yields on the back
of the French crisis is already imposing a new restrictive monetary
policy stance. Or in other words, the bond market has taken over
the ECB’s job.
According to Christine Lagarde, risks to the eurozone’s growth
outlook remain tilted to the downside, while risks to the inﬂation
outlook are tilted to the upside. A combination of risks that clearly

complicates the ECB’s path ahead. This dilemma was underscored
by her observation that key developments since the September
meeting have been a further rise in energy prices and a worsening
of ﬁnancing conditions.
Despite these new complications, the ECB apparently debated a
rate hike today. However, the worsening of ﬁnancing conditions
took the option oﬀ the table.
Lagarde also emphasised the importance of indirect and second-
round eﬀects from higher energy prices. Currently, the ECB is not

seeing any additional second-round eﬀects, other than the ones
included in the September staﬀ projections.
ECB looks set to hike again in December
despite increasing stagﬂationary pressures
Looking ahead, there are three main parameters that, in our view,
will determine the ECB’s path going forward:
1. The length and intensity of the war in the Middle East. The
longer oil prices remain elevated, the higher the probability of
more rate hikes.
2. The knock-on eﬀects of higher energy prices. So far, there has

hardly been any pass-through of higher energy prices in the
rest of the economy. If this remains the case, the need for
further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t, the possibility of rate hikes
rises.
3. France. Any solution to France's current ﬁscal woes could bring
some relief to bond markets and allow the ECB to continue
hiking rates. Without any solution, bond markets have taken

over the ECB’s role, making rate hikes less likely (and less

With today’s comments, another rate hike at the ECB’s December

meeting is clearly in the oﬃng. It would require a sharp worsening
of the growth outlook to probably prevent the ECB from hiking once
again.
In short, with downside risks to the growth outlook and upside risks
to the inﬂation outlook, the ECB seems to be increasingly
concerned about stagﬂationary forces, without calling them
stagﬂationary. Remember back in April, Lagarde called ‘stagﬂation’
a ‘ﬂashy term’.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026

from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the 
quarterly estimate. Outside of a 0.09ppt contribution to the 
revision from holiday travel & accommodation (revised up from 
4.2% to 5.6%) all else was via small contributions for a large 
number of expenditure categories. This is why there is a 0.2ppt 
increase in our September quarter TM estimate from 0.7%qtr 
to 0.9%qtr.
A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve

level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer

Manpreet Gill

Portfolio Strategist

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.
Fig. 3
Sukuk market size in USD bn

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

considered relatively less risky than conventional bonds. However, they are not
immune to risks. Unique challenges such as asset quality deterioration and
structure-specific exposures require rigorous risk management.

Due diligence in Sukuk extends beyond traditional credit
analysis and an investor should also consider the following:
• Shariah compliance verification can be done by evaluating

the Sukuk structure and confirming that its mandates and
underlying assets fully align with Islamic principles.
• Evaluating asset quality by verifying tangibility, cash -
generating capability and legal ownership can help
investor reduce risk.
• Assessing creditworthiness and market depth will help in
identifying financially strong Sukuk issuers and ensuring
adequate liquidity.
• Cross-border Sukuk investments remain viable,
contingent on careful scrutiny of legal documentation

across multiple jurisdictions to ensure enforceability and
investor protection.
Robust due diligence and proactive risk mitigation strategies
are essential to protect capital and stay aligned with Islamic
financial principles.
1 Risk management and due diligence

### Page 10

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  10

Staying up to date on the latest developments in 
the Sukuk market is important as this space is 
constantly evolving and rapidly expanding. 
Moreover, Islamic finance is not static; Shariah 
interpretations related to Sukuk structures are 
periodically redefined. Staying informed protects 
investors from investing in non -compliant 
options. We look at some of the factors that are 
guiding the future of this landscape:

1 Emerging trends

Green Sukuk issuance is projected to grow materially through 
2030, reflecting the natural convergence of Islamic finance and 
sustainability principles. Key sectors include renewable 
energy, green buildings, low -carbon transportation, water and 
waste management and sustainable agriculture.

Tech advancements  are revolutionising Sukuk origination, 
trading and accessibility. Digitisation offers numerous benefits 
to the Islamic finance landscape: streamlined issuance and 
trading processes, enhanced accessibility through 
tokenisation, immutable audit trails via bl ockchain technology 
and improved secondary market liquidity.

A major regulatory shift is likely to create standardised rules 
across Islamic finance markets globally. The pivotal 
development is AAOIFI's Standard 62, launching in 2026, 
which mandates transparent, Shariah-compliant asset-backing 
and genuine asset ownership. This can fundament ally 
strengthen the Islamic authenticity of Sukuk structures.

The Sukuk market is rapidly expanding beyond its traditional 
Islamic finance strongholds. Non-traditional territories  in 
Africa (e.g., Algeria, Benin and Nigeria), Europe (e.g., 
Luxemburg, UK) and Asia (e.g., Turkey, Pakistan) are 
emerging as Islamic capital market gateways. This expansion 
underscores the evolution of Sukuk from a niche Islamic 
product to a globally reco gnised asset class attracting diverse 
sovereigns and investors across continents.

### Page 11

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  11

Conclusion

Ultimately, Sukuk demonstrate that Shariah compliance and sound investment 
strategy are not competing priorities, but complementary ones. By ensuring that 
portfolios are grounded in real economic activity by offering stability through 
tangible asset -backing and transparent structures that channel capital towards 
productive use. Their foundation in ethical, responsible financing appeals to 
investors seeking both resilience and purpose. As global markets increasingly 
prioritise integrity and long-term sustainability, Sukuk stand out as a compelling tool 
for Shariah -compliant investors who want meaningful diversification without 
compromising on their religious and ethical principles.

US MARKET CALL: AI Spooking
Investors
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 31 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by David Menidrey / Unsplash

I. Stock Market Performance
Happy Halloween! Kids are enjoying the spooky cultural holiday today.
The celebration evolved from ancient Celtic harvest festivals tied to All
Hallows' Eve into the secular traditions of costumes, parties, and trick-or-
treating that we know today.

### Page 2

Investors have been spooked about the AI trade since the S&P 500
peaked at a record high of 7818.93 on October 6 (chart). The index is
down 3.1% since then. We can blame the big-cap AI-related stocks for
that decline because the equal-weight S&P 500—which doesn’t reflect
those stocks’ performance more than any other’s—is up 4.9% since
September 30. That's not very scary. Before that, the equal-weight index
had fallen fell 6.5% from its record high on August 13 through September

30.
The Nasdaq is down 7.8% since it peaked at a record high on October 6
(chart). That's not a very frightful decline for this relatively volatile index.
Somewhat more frightening has been the sharp drop in the momentum
trade relative to the S&P 500 since June 25 (chart). The former is a proxy

### Page 3

for the AI trade.
On a ytd basis, it has been mostly a walk past the graveyard for most
stock investors. For many, it has been a walk in the park. The S&P 500 is
up 10.7% since the end of 2025, with the XMAGS up 14.0%, while the
MAGS has lagged with a 3.8% gain so far this year (chart). So, despite
the frightening warnings about concentration of leadership, the S&P 500's
rally has broadened beyond the Magnificent-7.
Furthermore, the Russell 2000 has been on an uptrend since early 2025,

rising to a record high on August 14 (chart). It dropped 3.2% since then
through October 16, mostly on a scare from the bond market, which
seems to be over.

### Page 4

II. Stock Market Earnings
Meanwhile, industry analysts' consensus earnings expectations for the
S&P 500 show nothing to fear but fear itself. The real concern is that they
are not only fearless but also too optimistic. They currently expect that
EPS will increase 16.1% next year to $425 and 16.2% in 2028 to $494
(chart).
Occasionally, reality bites, and last week's Q3 earnings misses by
Alphabet and Intel modestly reduced the quarter's expected EPS growth

rate to 23.8%, but Q4's estimate remained exceptionally strong at 30.2%
(chart).

### Page 5

Forward earnings for the S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 all rose to
record highs again last week (chart).
III. Valuation
Investors do seem to be fretting that industry analysts’ earnings outlooks
reflect irrational exuberance. As a result, the S&P 500 forward P/E is down
to 18.2, its lowest reading since the April 2025 Liberation Day low (chart).
Forward earnings is up 49.1% since then! (The stock market rally since
then is all attributable to fabulous earnings momentum, or FEMO,

because FOMO, or the fear of losing out that drives up the “P” of “P/E,”
isn’t a factor.)
We still counsel investors to proceed with caution in the coming days.
Let's see how the midterm elections play out on November 3 and whether

### Page 6

the war in the Middle East heats up again soon after. Now that the FOMC
blackout period is over, Fed officials are likely to signal a bigger rate hike
in December than expected, say 50bps rather than 25bps. AI jitters are
likely to continue after Halloween. Nevertheless, we are sticking with our
7,900 year-end target for the S&P 500. Santa is coming.
IV. Sentiment
Odds are that Friday's drop in the S&P 500 weighed on the bull/bear ratios
(BBR) we track (chart). We hope so. From a contrarian perspective,

bearish BBR readings are bullish.
V. Credit

### Page 7

Alphabet and Intel stock prices dropped sharply after they reported
weaker-than-expected earnings last week. As a result, investors pulled
back from the AI trade on mounting fears that the AI infrastructure buildout
is at risk of a significant slowdown. That notion spread to fixed-income
securities. In recent weeks, the federal funds futures market's forecast of
the number of Fed rate cuts over the next 12 months fell from three or
more to two (chart).

The 10-year Treasury bond yield dropped by more than 30bps this month,
led by a similar drop in the comparable TIPS yield (chart). We think that
the 10-year yield might remain around 5.00% through the end of this year
and for most of next year.

市場投資展望
《臉書 Muse 點燃追趕行情，谷歌困於模型商品化
與旗艦空窗》
臉書代理式 AI 產品 Muse 迅速爆紅，推動股價單日
創 13 個月來最佳表現。市場開始將臉書從「AI 資
本支出大戶」重新定位為「具備自有 AI 爆款產品的
平台」。月底臉書乘勝進軍企業 AI 平台。然而，AI
代理競爭仍在初期 ，臉書雖挾分發優勢 ，但變現仍
待觀察。谷歌則在法務與搜尋基本面接連傳出利多 。
然而，OpenAI 與 Anthropic 接連大砍 API 價格導
致 AI 推理商品化疑慮，加上旗艦模型 Gemini 4 仍

遲遲未推出 ，谷歌股價因此持續承壓 。資本支出強
度攀升導致的 ROIC 走弱的核心敘事尚未見到反轉 ，
因此通訊服務仍維持中性看待。
• 9 月臉書推出面向消費端的 AI
代理 Muse，正面回應市場持
續關注的變現疑問。
• 臉書近期也成立企業 AI 平台，
將代理人技術延伸至企業端。
• 臉書成功回應市場對臉書有無
AI 應用產品的疑慮。但投入回
收成效仍待進一步驗證。
• 谷歌整合式廣告業務營運未受
結構性衝擊，搜尋有自 AI 競
爭者收復失土的跡象。
• 但谷歌在 AI 模型層仍面臨 AI
兩大壓力，不利市場信心。
1
資產 4Q26 看法 投資展望
通訊服務 中立 通訊服務主成分臉書近期推出消費端 AI 代理 Muse 與企業 AI 平台，
成功回應市場對於「臉書有沒有 AI 產品」的疑問，然後續 AI 投資變
現結果仍待逐步驗證。谷歌流失在廣告與搜尋業務迎來好消息，然在
AI 模型端面臨雙重逆風，不利市場信心。本行預期市場仍將聚焦檢視
資本支出效率與變現進度。靜候 10 月財報結果，維持中立評等。

資料來源：Koyfin、Capital IQ，2026/09/29；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09/29。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要
零售客流量表
• 9 月臉書推出面向消費端的 AI
代理 Muse，正面回應市場持
續關注的變現疑問。
• 臉書近期也宣布成立企業 AI
平台，以圖將代理人技術從消
費端延伸至企業端。
• 臉書成功回應市場對臉書有無
AI 應用產品的疑慮。但投入回
收成效仍待進一步驗證。
2
《臉書 AI 投資變現路徑初具雛形，消費端與企業端
同步開展》
9 月臉書推出面向消費端的 AI 代理 Muse，正面回
應市場持續關注的「臉書能否以自有 AI 產品在 40
億用戶基礎上變現」的疑問。Muse 是能代替用戶
執行比價、訂購、換約等具體任務的個人代理人。
上市後迅速登上美國 App Store 與 Google Play 下
載榜首，印證臉書既有社群入口與用戶關係在 AI 產

品分發上的結構性優勢。Muse 雖為臉書打開訂閱、
交易與導購分潤、廣告業務強化三條潛在變現路徑，
但其成敗仍取決於 Muse 能否延續上線初期的動能、
擴展至美國以外市場，並深入用戶日常使用情境。
同時，Muse 的代理能力正引發平台間的摩擦。亞
馬遜隨即封鎖其代理購物功能，拒絕第三方代理人
直接在其平台上替用戶下單，銀行、保險、旅遊等
仰賴消費者惰性賺取溢價的產業也可能採取類似的
防禦措施，交易分潤路徑能否成立，將取決於臉書
與既有平台的合作或議價能力。
緊接在 Muse 之後，臉書近期也宣布成立企業 AI 平
台，並延攬 MongoDB 執行長 CJ Desai 領導此一新
事業，以圖將代理人技術從消費端延伸至企業端。
臉書在企業市場的最大資產是全球大量中小企業已
用來與客戶溝通的 WhatsApp。在 WhatsApp 基礎
上衍生的自動處理詢價、下單、售後客服的企業 AI
代理應用具有相當的想像空間。不過，本行認為臉
書企業平台執行的不確定性明顯高於 Muse。主要
仍是考量到中小企業的數據品質、客戶信任與資安

風險與消費端截然不同。臉書過去缺乏企業銷售團
隊與服務紀錄，從零建立企業級通路需要時間與額
外投入，或將加重臉書的資本與費用負擔。
因此，本行認為 Muse 僅回應了市場對臉書 AI 投資
變現的第一個問題「臉書有沒有 AI 產品」。第二個
問題「投入何時能回收」，仍有待後續進展驗證。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要
零售客流量表
• 谷歌整合式廣告業務營運未受
結構性衝擊，搜尋有自 AI 競
爭者收復失土的跡象。
• 但谷歌在 AI 模型層仍面臨 AI
推理商品化與旗艦模型空窗期
的壓力，不利市場信心。
3
《谷歌核心業務韌性無虞，但 AI 變現面臨商品化與
旗艦空窗雙重逆風》
9 月初聯邦法官駁回美國司法部拆分谷歌廣告技術
業務的訴求，改採行為矯正措施，意味谷歌廣告技
術堆疊得以保持完整，整合式廣告業務的營運模式
不受結構性衝擊，延續上半年 Android 應用商店案
和解落幕後法務風險持續收斂的趨勢。搜尋方面，
業界觀察顯示谷歌搜尋已在 ChatGPT 等 AI 競爭者
挑戰下收復部分使用量，AI Overviews 與 AI Mode

似乎有效留住用戶，緩解了 AI 聊天機器人侵蝕搜尋
入口的疑慮。
然而，谷歌在 AI 模型層面臨兩項壓力。第一是 AI
推理商品化。9 月下旬 OpenAI 推出 GPT-6 Sol 與
Luna 並將 API 價格砍半，Anthropic 亦發表 Opus
5.5，效能比肩前代頂級模型、成本卻降低 40%，並
同步上架三大雲端平台。前沿模型價格快速下探，
意味 Google Cloud 的 AI 推理營收單價與 Gemini
API 的定價空間在資本支出擴張之時同步受壓。價
格下行對使用量的影響動態將是市場在 10 月財報時
的觀察重點。
第二個壓力是旗艦模型的持續空窗。9 月下旬
DeepMind 新任主管暗示 Gemini 4 可望於年底前
推出，意味谷歌在 26Q4 之前將持續缺席前沿旗艦
競爭，但而同一期間 OpenAI 已推出 GPT-6、
Anthropic 推出 Opus 5.5。旗艦模型能力雖不必然
保證應用端的落地成效，但 Flash 等輕量模型與法
律、金融等垂直版本，多以旗艦模型為基礎蒸餾或

衍生，旗艦進度因此直接決定整體模型組合所能涵
蓋的應用場景廣度與深度。在上有閉源前沿模型、
下有低價開放權重模型持續滲透企業市場的夾擊下，
旗艦模型進度落後將持續壓抑市場對谷歌雲在企業
AI 應用的看法與信心。

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 28 日

1/3 
タイ中銀、インフレ加速も景気支援を重視して４会合連続で金利据え置き 
～原油高やバーツ安など物価上昇リスクがくすぶるなか、難しい政策運営が続くであろう～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ タイ銀行（中央銀行）は、10 月 28 日に開催した定例の金融政策委員会において、政策金利
を４会合連続で1.00％に据え置くことを決定した。 同国では、 イラン情勢の悪化による原油
高などを背景にインフレが加速しているものの、内需の弱さを反映して需要面からの物価上
昇圧力は限定的とみられる。 
○ 一方で、世界的なＡＩ関連需要の拡大が一部産業を押し上げているものの、家計債務の大き
さや雇用環境の脆弱さなどが個人消費の重しとなり、景気回復の恩恵は一部にとどまってい
るとの見方を示している。さらに、原油高による対外収支の悪化や洪水対策に伴う財政負担
の増大、通貨バーツ安なども景気や物価に対する不透明感を強めている。 
○ こうしたなか、中銀は足元の金利水準が景気回復を支えるうえで適切との認識を示し、イン
フレの上昇は一時的との見方を維持するなど、景気支援を優先する姿勢を示した。ただし、
エルニーニョ現象やイラン情勢を巡る不透明感に加え、金融緩和の長期化に伴うリスクも意
識されるなか、先行きの政策運営は引き続き難しい局面が続くと見込まれる。

タイ銀行（中央銀行）は、10 月 28 日に開催した定例の金融政策委員会において、政策金利を４
会合連続で1.00％に据え置くことを決定した。同行は、 イラン情勢が緊迫化する直前に開催した２
月会合において、同国経済のコロナ禍からの回復が ASEAN（東南アジア諸国連合）主要国のなかで
も遅れていることに加え、 物価の低迷が続いていることなどを背景に利下げに踏み切った。しかし、
直後にイラン情勢が緊迫化したことで状況は一変した。その結果、 中銀は金利を据え置く様子見姿
勢を維持している。

### Page 2

2/3 
タイでは、2025 年半ば以降のインフレ率はマイナスで推移していたものの、2026 年４月以降は一
転してプラスに転じている。 直近９月の
インフレ率は前年同月比＋ 2.82％と中
銀の目標レンジ （２±１％） 内にとどま
るものの、 上限に近付くなどインフレ圧
力が強まっている様子がうかがえる （図
１） 。コアインフレ率は前年同月比＋
1.50％にとどまるものの、2023 年５月以
来の高い伸びとなるなど、 インフレの動
きが広がりをみせている可能性がある。
とはいえ、 コアインフレ率は依然として
低水準にあり、 需要面からの物価上昇圧
力も限定的とみられることが、中銀の様子見姿勢を後押ししたと考えられる。 
金融市場においては、原油高が対外収支を悪化させるとともに、政府による家計支援を目的とし
た補助金政策などに伴う財政悪化への懸念も重なり、経済のファンダメンタルズ（基礎的条件）の
脆弱化が警戒されてきた。さらに、タイでは９月中旬以降、豪雨による洪水被害が広がり、政府は
洪水対策を目的 に財政 支援を拡充し た
ため、 公的債務残高のＧＤＰ比は法定上
限（70％） に近付いている （注１） 。 こう
した事情も重なり、 通貨バーツ相場は調
整の動きを強めて いる（図２） 。タイで
は、伝統的に金取引が活発であり、バー
ツ相場は 年明け直後にかけて金価格に
連れ高する形で強含んだ。しかし、足元
では金価格が調整の動きを強めており、
こうした動きも バーツ安を後押しして
いる可能性がある。バーツ安は輸出競争
力を高める一方、 輸入物価の上昇を通じてインフレ圧力を増幅させることが懸念される。 とはいえ、
足元では景気の不透明要因が山積しており、中銀にとっては様子見姿勢を維持せざるを得ない事情
もうかがえる 
会合後に公表した声明文では、今回の決定が「全会一致（出席した６名の政策委員が据え置きを
支持し、 １名が欠席）」 であったことを明らかにした。 タイ経済については 「ＡＩ （人工知能） 関連
の好循環が景気の勢いを増している」 ものの、 「経済成長率は潜在成長率を下回り、 景気拡大の恩恵

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
3/3
は一部に集中している」との認識を示した。一方で、物価動向については「供給要因を理由に一時
的な上昇が見込まれる」 とした。 信用動向については 「全体的な伸びは加速している」 ものの、 「中
小企業向け融資は引き続き縮小している」ほか、 「中小企業や家計向け融資の質を注視する必要が
ある」との認識を示した。そのうえで、中銀の政策運営について「緩和的な金融政策と的を絞った
金融措置は景気回復を下支えしている」 と評価しつつ、 「金利を据え置き、 イラン情勢やエルニーニ
ョ現象によるインフレリスクの影響を注視する必要がある」とした。
具体的には、 「2026 年の経済成長率は＋2.4％、2027 年は＋2.1％になる」 との見通しを示す一方、

「その恩恵はＡＩに関連する製造業やサービス業の企業・労働者に集中している」とした。個人消
費については「家計債務の大きさ、脆弱な雇用環境、低所得世帯を中心とする購買力の弱さが重し
になっている」との見方を示した。加えて、「原油高が外国人観光客数の足かせになる」としたほ
か、「全体としての経済成長が潜在成長率を下回るなかで、中小企業は依然事業環境への適応や競
争激化という困難に直面している」との認識を示した。
物価動向については「2026 年のインフレ率は＋2.1％、2027 年は＋1.3％になる」との見通しを
示し、 「エネルギー価格やエルニーニョ現象の影響で 2027 年初めにかけて上昇が続く」一方、 「そ
の後は構造的な制約要因による低成長や内需の弱さを反映して鈍化する」 とした。 コアインフレ率
も「2026 年は＋1.2％、2027 年は＋1.3％になる」との見通しを示すとともに、 「中期的なインフレ
期待は依然として目標レンジ内に定着している」 とした。 ただし、 「イラン情勢の動向、 補助金財政

の余力低下、 企業によるコスト転嫁の影響、 中期的なインフレ期待による上振れリスクを注視する」
とした。
バーツ相場については「ＦＲＢ（米連邦準備制度理事会）による引き締め観測や原油高が重しに
なっている」 とした。 一方で、 「国際的な金利上昇の動きにあわせてタイでも長期金利が上昇してい
る」ものの、 「民間部門の借入コストは概ね安定している」ほか、「大企業向けをけん引役に信用動
向は回復しているが、 中小企業向け融資は縮小している」、「中小企業や低所得者層をはじめ家計の
債務返済能力を注視する必要がある」との認識を示した。
先行きの政策運営については「物価安定と持続可能な経済成長、金融安定の維持を目指す」との
従来からの考え方を繰り返した。 そのうえで、 「足元の金利水準が景気回復を支えるうえで適切」と
して、景気支援を優先する考えを示した。物価動向については「供給要因による物価は上昇してい
るが、 今後もインフレ見通しや関連するリスクを注視する」 と８月の前回会合と同じ文言を繰り返

すなど、インフレが一時的なものにとどまるとの認識を維持している。一方、今回は「低金利が長
期的なマクロ金融安定に及ぼす影響」 にも言及するなど、 金融緩和の長期化に伴うリスクにも一定
の警戒姿勢を示した。とはいえ、 先行きはエルニーニョ現象の影響が長期化する懸念が高まってお
り、 世界経済や国際金融市場を巡る不透明要因も山積するなか、 政策の舵取りは引き続き難しい局
面が続くと考えられる。
以 上

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 30 日

1/6 
中共５中全会閉幕、次期党大会を見据えて党・軍への統制強化を加速 
～経済政策の実効性と日中関係の行方にも注目～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 中国では、10 月26～29 日に中国共産党の第20 期中央委員会第５回全体会議（５中全会）が
開催された。今回は、従来の慣例とは異なり、「全面的かつ厳格な党内統治」が主要議題と
なり、党規律や幹部の監督、反腐敗の強化、党中央による政策履行の徹底などを盛り込んだ
決定が採択された。また、軍幹部を含む多数の党籍剥奪処分を追認するなど、党・軍に対す
る統制強化の姿勢が鮮明となった。さらに、2027 年後半に第 21 回党大会を開催することが
正式に決定され、習近平指導部は次期党大会に向けて組織・人事面での基盤固めを進める方
針を示したと考えられる。 
○ 一方で、経済政策面では、内需拡大や景気安定に向けた方針が再確認されたものの、具体的
な追加刺激策は示されなかった。足元の中国経済は、外需の堅調さを背景に持ち直しの動き
がみられる一方、不動産不況や消費・投資の低迷など、内需の弱さが依然として課題となっ
ている。こうしたなか、今後は既存の景気支援策の実効性が焦点となるほか、党内統治の厳
格化が行政の政策遂行能力を高めるのか、一方で幹部の責任回避などによる政策執行の遅れ
を招くのかが注目される。 
○ 日本への影響については、中国の内需回復が日本企業の輸出や現地販売を下支えする可能性
がある一方、 中国政府による国産化や産業育成の推進が競争圧力を強めることも懸念される。
さらに、重要部材の輸出管理強化や台湾情勢を巡る安全保障上の不確実性が、日本企業の調
達や事業活動に及ぼす影響にも注意が必要となる。 今後は、2027 年の次期党大会に向けた組
織・人事の動向に加え、景気支援策の具体的な執行状況、地方行政の対応、対外関係の変化
などを通じて、習近平指導部の政策運営の方向性を見極める必要がある。

【今回の５中全会では、党内統治の強化と政策の履行徹底が主要議題となった】 
中国では、10 月 26 日から 29 日までの日程で中国共産党の第 20 期中央委員会第５回全体会議
（５中全会） が開催された。党大会の閉会期間中に党の指導機関としての役割を担う中央委員会は、
５年の任期中にほぼ１年ごとに１回全体会議を開催し、 党の統治や運営上の重要政策、 人事などの
議題を討議する。１中全会では新指導部が正式に発足し、２中全会では国務院（政府）などの人事

### Page 2

2/6 
案を承認し、３中全会では経済政策を討議するなど、２中全会と３中全会は通常、党大会の翌年に
開催される。 しかし、 習近平指導部は３中全会を2023 年に開催せず、2024 年７月に後ずれした（注
１）。さらに、2025 年 10 月に開始した４中全会では、第 15 次５ヵ年計画（十五五）が討議された
（注２）。 
その結果、通常は５ヵ年計画を討議する場となる５中全会で、今回は「全面的かつ厳格な党内統
治 （全面従厳治党） 」 が主要議題となり、 実質的に党大会の前年に開催される６中全会に近い性格を
帯びることとなった。このように、習近平指導部の下では、党の会議の開催時期や議題などについ
て、過去の慣例にとらわれない動きがみられる。 
会議では、 習氏が中央政治局を代表して行った活動報告を聴取 ・ 討議するとともに、 「全面的かつ
厳格な党内統治を持続的に推進するうえでの若干の重大問題に関する決定」 を採択した。同決定に
は、党規律、幹部の選抜・監督、組織運営、反腐敗の強化といった内容が盛り込まれた。会議後に
公表された公報によれば、 中央の指示を地方や各部門まで徹底するとともに、 政治的な忠誠と実務
上の遂行能力を幹部管理に反映させる姿勢がうかがえる。 反腐敗を長期的な執政の基盤と位置付け
たうえで、 個々の処分にとどまらず、 組織的な監督と責任追及の仕組みを整える狙いがあると考え
られる。 
この方向性を考えるうえでは、会議に先立って党の理論誌『求是』に掲載された習氏の演説が重
要な手がかりとなる。これは１月に行われた演説の主要部分を公表したものであり、 このなかで習
氏は、党中央の決定が実行されない、または恣意的に実行される問題を挙げるとともに、政治的な
監督の強化の必要性を訴えた。このため、今回の会議においても、政治的忠誠と政策履行の徹底、
反腐敗と監督の制度化、幹部の評価・登用の厳格化が重視されたと考えられる。 
人事面では、中央軍事委員会の前副主席である張又侠氏や元委員の劉振立氏など 33 人に対する
党籍剥奪処分を追認したほか、 別の１人に対する党内職務の罷免処分を決定した。 党籍剥奪の対象
者は、 習近平指導部の下で開催された中央委員会全体会議として過去最多の規模とみられ、 うち16
人は元軍関係者が占めた。軍幹部を巡っては、ここ数年の相次ぐ処分が指揮命令系統の弱体化を招
くと懸念されてきた。このため、中央軍事委員会の欠員補充が注目されたものの、今回は同委員会
副主席の張昇民氏を中央政治局委員に追加選出するにとどまった。軍の規律検査を担ってきた張氏
を昇格させることで、 次期党大会に向けて軍への統制を強化し、 指導体制の立て直しを進める方針
が示されたと考えられる。 とはいえ、 軍の指揮命令系統を巡る空洞化の問題は残されたままである。
一方、 今回は中央委員24 人が党籍剥奪処分の対象となるなか、 中央候補委員10 人を中央委員に繰
り上げるなど、組織体制の補強も図られた。こうした動きを踏まえれば、今回の５中全会は党・軍
への引き締めを一段と強化する会議となったと捉えられる。

注１ 2024 年７月 19 日付レポート「中国･３中全会､習近平氏が掲げる｢中国式現代化｣の加速を推進」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/351237.html） 
注２ 2025 年10 月24 日付レポート「中共４中全会閉幕､中長期的な国力向上の姿勢が鮮明に」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/530799.html）

### Page 3

3/6

【2027 年に開催予定の次期党大会に向けて、組織・人事面での基盤整備に注力】 
５中全会では、中国共産党第 21 回全国代表大会（第 21 回党大会）を 2027 年後半に北京で開催
することを正式に決定した。これにより、今後行われる中央・地方の幹部人事や政策運営を評価す
るうえでは、次期党大会に向けた準備という視点がこれまで以上に重要になる。 
５中全会での決定事項を踏まえれば、政治的忠誠と政策遂行能力の重視という観点が、 次の指導
部を担う幹部の評価と選抜に関わると考えられる。次期党大会に向けて地方・中央の人事が進めら
れる過程では、党中央の方針をどれだけ着実に実行できるかが問われることになろう。ただし、今
回行われた中央委員の補充は党規約に定められた手続きに沿ったものであり、今回補充された 10
人がそのまま後継指導者候補とはならないことに注意が必要である。 
さらに、思想面から現行指導体制の継続を支える動きも重要であろう。具体的には、公報におい
て、毛沢東思想や鄧小平理論など従来の指導思想に言及するなかで「習近平党建設思想」が明記さ
れ、習近平「新時代の中国の特色ある社会主義」思想の重要な構成要素と位置付けられたことは、
同指導部による党運営の理念を定着させる動きと捉えられる。 同思想は、 「人民の利益を重視する
（人民至上） 」立場を基本としつつ、党統治の厳格化、 反腐敗運動の徹底、 民主集中制の強化など、
今回の５中全会で討議された内容と重なる要素が多い。なお、この概念は 2026 年６月に開催され
た全国党建設活動座談会で正式に提起・体系化されており、今回初めて登場したものではない。今
後は、次期党大会の政治報告や党規約などにどのような形で反映されるかが注目される。 
前述したように、 ５中全会では多数の軍幹部が処分されたものの、これは規律による統制を徹底
する姿勢と、人事面での再整備を続ける必要性の双方を示唆している。今回、中央政治局委員に選
出された張升民氏は軍の規律検査を担ってきた経歴を有しており、習近平指導部が人民解放軍に対
する政治的統制を重視してきた姿勢と整合的である。 一方で、軍人事の変更だけで軍の実戦能力や
対外行動を直接判断することは難しい。 習氏は、 台湾統一を目的とする武力行使を選択肢から排除
しておらず、 米軍に対抗し得る『世界一流の軍隊』の建設を目指してきた。2027 年８月には人民解
放軍の創設100 周年を迎えることから、 それまでに軍の指揮命令系統の再構築が進めば、 同年後半
の党大会で４期目入りを目指すとみられる習氏にとって、 権力基盤の一層の安定につながる可能性
がある。

【経済政策面では具体策に乏しいものの、景気安定に向けた政策の実行力が焦点に】 
一方で、経済政策との関係では、十五五の策定過程との区別が必要である。十五五に関する「建
議」は４中全会で採択されるとともに、それを具体化した「綱要」は2026 年３月の全人代（第 14
期全国人民代表大会第４回全体会議）で承認されている （注３）。このため、今回の５中全会は、 こ
うした既存の政策を着実に遂行する組織・幹部の規律を整えるための会議と位置付けられる。

注３ ３月５日付レポート「中国､2026 年全人代開幕､特異性を強める経済､内政､外交」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/581059.html）

### Page 4

4/6 
こうしたなか、 公報では、マクロ政策の活用と内需拡大を求めるとともに、2026 年の経済・社会
発展目標を達成する方針を再確認した。一方で、追加財政支出の規模や利下げ幅、新たな家計給付
の金額など、具体的な政策措置は示されなかった。今後の政策運営の動きについては、政府や中国
人民銀行（中銀）をはじめとする金融当局が発表する政策の内容に加え、その執行状況などを確認
したうえで評価する必要があると考えられる。 
足元の中国経済を巡っては、経済成長率に持ち直しの動きがみられる一方、 内需は力強さを欠く
推移をみせている。 ７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前年同期比＋4.5％と前期 （同＋4.3％） から小幅
に加速したほか、前期比年率ベースでは＋4.9％と試算されるなど持ち直しの動きが確認されてい
る（注４）。一方で、９月までの累計ベースの小売売上高（社会消費品小売総額）は前年比＋1.1％
にとどまり、固定資産投資は同▲7.2％とマイナスで推移するとともに、なかでも不動産投資は同
▲20.6％と大幅に下振れしている。 こうしたなか、 足元の景気は外需の旺盛な動きを追い風にした
ハイテク製造業を中心とする供給サイドがけん引役となる展開が続いており、国内の需給不均衡が
解消されない状況にある。国家統計局も、内需の弱さを経済運営上の課題として認識しており、景
気の回復力にはなお弱さが残ると考えられる。 
このため、 金融市場では、2027 年後半に開催される党大会を前に、 景気や雇用、 社会の安定を維
持する観点から、何らかの政策対応が強化されるとの見方が根強い。とはいえ、内需の低迷が長期
化しているにもかかわらず、 これまで大規模な景気刺激策に積極姿勢をみせてこなかったことを勘
案すれば、政治日程のみを材料に積極姿勢に転じると見込むことは難しい。国務院は７月、同国で
初めて個人消費に焦点を当てた 「消費拡大の５ヵ年計画」 を公表した。同計画では、2030 年の小売
売上高（社会消費品小売総額）の規模を2025 年（50.1 兆元）に比べて約２割（60 兆元前後）引き
上げるべく、消費構造の高度化や家計所得の引き上げを目指すとした。加えて、共産党は７月末に
開催した中央政治局会議において、 インフラ関連など財政支出の進捗加速による景気下支えを図る
方針を示した。これを受けて、政府・金融当局は８月以降、内需促進を目的とする新たな財政・金
融支援策を発表しており、今後はこれらの政策がどの程度の効果を上げるかを見定める必要がある。 
一方で、今後焦点となり得るのが、党内統治の厳格化と政策実行力の関係であろう。汚職や形式
的な行政対応が減るとともに、党中央・政府が提示した内需支援策が適切に執行されれば、政策の
実効性が高まることが期待される。ただし、幹部が責任追及を警戒して判断を先送りするほか、上
位機関への照会を増やすような動きが広がれば、 企業の許認可や政策資金の執行が遅れる可能性は
残る。 今回の決定のみをもって政策効果の行方を判断することはできず、 地方レベルでの行政実務
の動向を注視する必要があろう。その意味では、政策の規模に加え、資金の使途と執行状況を点検
することが重要になる。仮に、今後も供給サイドの改革が優先されるとともに、産業設備投資への
支援が集中すれば、需要に見合わない生産能力の増強を招くとともに、国内における価格競争や対
外摩擦を強める可能性に注意が必要となる。

注４ 10 月19 日付レポート「中国・７-９月GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666711.html）

### Page 5

5/6

【日本への影響を巡っては、中国の内需動向、行政運営、産業競争、供給網などの動向に注意】 
日本にとって中国は、 需要先であると同時に重要な供給元でもある。2025 年時点において、日本
の貿易に占める中国の割合は、輸出で 17.0％、輸入で 23.6％となっている。中国の需要動向は日
本の輸出や現地販売に、供給面の変化は日本の調達や生産活動に影響を及ぼす。このため、５中全
会で示された党内統治の強化の行方が、今後どのような形で政策運営や行政実務に反映されるかが
重要になる。 
まず、内需の行方は、中国に進出する日系企業の販売機会に直結すると予想される。当局が主導
する家計所得や雇用への支援が実効性を持つことができれば、 消費関連の日系企業の現地販売のほ
か、 日本からの輸出を支えることが期待される。一方で、産業設備への投資が中心となる場合には、
機械・電子部品・素材などに需要が向かいやすい。ただし、支援対象、現地調達条件、日本企業の
競争力によって恩恵は異なると予想されるほか、 中国の景気対策の規模がそのまま日本の輸出増に
つながるとは限らない点には注意が必要である。 
次に、 行政運営が事業環境に及ぼす影響にも注意が必要となる。 規律強化が恣意的な行政や不透
明な取引を減らせば、企業の事業環境に対する予見性を高める効果が期待できる。反対に、担当者
の交代や責任追及への警戒が判断の遅れを招く場合には、事業認可、政府調達、契約履行などに支
障が生じ得る。中国日本商会が発表した 2026 年白書においても、行政手続の迅速化、制度運用の
統一、内外企業の公平な競争条件の確保などが求められている。日本企業にとっては、こうした改
善が実務レベルでどの程度進展するかが重要な判断材料になる。 
さらに、中国国内における産業育成に伴う需要と競争環境の行方にも注意が必要となる。習近平
指導部が掲げる科学技術の自立自強やサプライチェーンの強靱化、 「新質生産力」の育成といった
方針は、すでに十五五の策定段階で重視されている。今後、こうした動きが一段と強力に推進され
れば、日本の設備・部材メーカーに販売機会をもたらすことが期待される一方、国産化が進んだ製
品については中期的に競争圧力が強まる可能性がある。すでに、 太陽光電池や風力発電設備につい
ては、中国メーカーが世界市場で高いシェアを有するほか、ＥＶ（電気自動車）でも高い国際競争
力を持つ。中国市場における需要の行方だけでなく、日本企業の技術や製品が代替される可能性の
ほか、第三国市場での競争激化が一段と加速する可能性にも注意が必要である。さらに、世界的に
ＡＩ（人工知能） ・半導体を巡る開発競争が激化するなか、７月には中国が主導して世界ＡＩ機構
（WAICO） という国際機関を設立するなど、 この分野をリードする姿勢を強めており、 その動向にも
注意が必要である。 
足元で特に注視すべきなのが、 重要部材の供給と安全保障を巡る影響であろう。 このところの中
国は、高市首相による台湾有事を巡る国会答弁や日本の防衛政策に対する反発を強めるなか、日本
に対する 「経済的威圧」 を強めている。 中国商務部は１月､日本に対する軍民両用品（デュアルユー
ス）の輸出管理を強化する方針を発表し、その後も対象を拡大するなど、規制を強化してきた。足
元では、 レアアース関連の輸出許可や通関手続きの運用を巡る不確実性が、 企業実務上の重要な課
題となっている。今後も、対象品目や審査基準に関する当局の個別公告に加え、企業による輸出許

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
可の取得や調達の動向を確認する必要があることは間違いない。 
安全保障面についても、 軍幹部の処分や政治局人事をもって、台湾有事の時期や可能性を判断す
ることは困難である。日本への影響については、台湾海峡や東シナ海における実際の軍事活動、日
中間の外交対話、経済措置の変化を通じて判断する必要がある。企業にとっては、物流、重要部材
の調達、 現地拠点の運営にどのような支障が生じ得るかを、 平時から具体的に点検することの重要
性がこれまで以上に高まっていると判断できる。

【今後は、政策の実施内容と現場レベルでの反応を確認する必要性が高まっている】 
先行きについては、 現行路線の継続と党大会に向けた景気安定重視が基本線となる一方、 行政の
実行力や対外関係の行方によって実際の展開が左右されると考えられる。とりわけ、 幹部人事と次
期指導部の形成過程、家計支援・不動産対策・産業支援の具体的内容と執行状況、日系企業の許認
可・代金回収・調達の実態、輸出管理や外交・安全保障上の具体的な変化を継続的に確認する必要
性は高まっている。次期党大会で示される政治報告と、 その後に開催される１中全会での指導部人
事の行方は、その先の政策運営を評価する重要な材料となる。その意味では、開催まで約１年とな
った次期党大会に向けて、 党中央と地方組織双方の動きをこれまで以上に注視する必要があると考
えられる。

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 19 日

1/6 
中国・７-９月 GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず 
～供給サイドが成長をけん引する一方、個人消費と不動産は力強さを欠く展開が続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 中国経済は、世界的なＡＩ・半導体需要を背景に輸出や製造業が堅調に推移する一方、不動
産不況や雇用回復の遅れを背景に個人消費や投資は力強さを欠いており、 「強さ」 と 「弱さ」
が混在している。７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前年比＋4.5％と前期から加速し、堅調な外
需を追い風に、製造業、鉱業が景気を下支えする動きが確認された。ただし、企業部門では
商品市況上昇によるコスト増が進む一方、家計の節約志向から最終価格への転嫁は難しく、
企業部門のインフレと消費者部門のディスインフレが併存している。 
○ 政府は個人消費喚起策や財政 ・ 金融支援を強化しており、 ９月の小売売上高（前年比＋1.1％）
には持ち直しの兆しがみられた。ただ、その中身をみると、低価格業態やＥＣへの需要シフ
トが進む一方、 自動車や宝飾品、 娯楽関連などの需要は弱く、 家計の節約志向は依然根強い。
不動産市場も大都市の一部で改善の兆しがみられるものの、地方都市では住宅価格の下落が
続き、不動産投資（年初来前年比▲20.6％）は歴史的な低水準にある。一方、設備投資や公
共投資も含む固定資産投資（年初来前年比▲7.2％）には底打ちの兆しもみられる。 
○ 供給面では、 ９月の鉱工業生産が前年比＋5.5％と加速し、外需の旺盛な動きを追い風にした
ハイテク製造業を中心に堅調さが続いている。内需が弱いなか、中国経済は引き続き外需と
供給サイドへの依存度が高く、国内の需給不均衡も続いている。加えて、原材料価格の上昇
を製品価格へ十分転嫁できず企業収益が圧迫されていることや、不動産規制への懸念から中
国本土株は上値の重い展開が続いている。先行きについては、政策効果の及び方によって業
種間の業績格差が一段と広がる可能性には引き続き注意が必要と考えられる。

【外需の堅調さをけん引役に、７-９月ＧＤＰは前年比＋4.5％に伸びが加速している】 
足元の中国経済を巡っては、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要の旺盛さを追い風に輸出
は堅調な動きをみせている。こうした動きを背景に、ＡＩ関連やＥＶ（電気自動車）など個別企業
は世界的な競争力を高めるなど、ミクロ面での強さがうかがえる。一方で、長引く不動産不況や若
年層を中心とする雇用回復の遅れなどが個人消費をはじめとする内需は力強さを欠いている。 この
ように、マクロ面でみた中国経済は弱さがうかがえるなど、相反する材料が混在している。

### Page 2

2/6 
こうした状況は、 足元の物価動向にも現れている。 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油をはじめとす
る商品市況の上昇を反映して、川上段階の企業部門はインフレに直面している。さらに、企業間取
引段階においては価格転嫁の動きが広がりをみせる流れも確認できる。 しかし、 家計部門が節約志
向を強めるなかで、 企業部門にとっては最終製品への価格転嫁のハードルが高い状況が続いている。
その結果、消費者段階の物価は低迷するディスインフレ基調が続いている。このように、足元の中
国経済はインフレとディスインフレが共存する展開となっている（注１）。 
このように、足元の中国経済は「強さ」と「弱さ」が混在する状況にあるなか、７-９月の実質Ｇ
ＤＰ成長率は前年同期比＋4.5％となり、 前期 （同＋4.3％） から加速した （図１）。 前期比の成長率
も＋1.2％と前期（同＋0.9％）から加速
しており、年率換算では＋ 4.9％と試算
されるなど持ち直しの動きが確認され
ている。分野ごとの動きをみると、個人
消費など内需の弱さを反映して第３次
産業（サービス業）は頭打ちする一方、
第１次産業（農林漁業）の生産は堅調に
推移しているほか、 第２次産業 （製造業 ・
鉱業） の生産が持ち直したことが足元の
景気を下支えしている。イラン情勢の悪
化による商品市況の上昇を受けて、 鉱業
部門の生産が活発化しているほか、 輸出の堅調さが製造業の生産を押し上げる展開が続く。 前述の
ように川上段階でのインフレを受けて、 名目成長率 （前年同期比＋6.0％） は２四半期連続で実質成
長率を上回る伸びとなるなど、マクロ的には「デフレ脱却」が進んでいる。

【個人消費に一見持ち直しの兆しも、その内容をみれば依然として厳しい状況が続いている】 
個人消費をはじめとする内需が力強さを欠くなか、国務院は７月、 同国で初めて個人消費に焦点
を当てた 「消費拡大の５ヵ年計画」を公表した。 同計画では、2030 年の小売売上高 （社会消費品小
売総額）の規模を2025 年（50.1 兆元）に比べて約２割（60 兆元前後）引き上げ、その実現へ消費
構造の高度化や家計所得の引き上げを目指すとした。 加えて、 共産党は７月末に開催した中央政治
局会議において、 インフラ関連など財政支出の進捗加速による景気下支えを図る方針を示した。 財
政部・中銀（中国人民銀行） ・国家金融監督管理総局は、８月17 日付で内需促進を目的とする新た
な財政・金融支援策を発表し、中小・零細民間企業や消費者向け融資への利子補給を拡充すること
を明らかにしている。 
こうした一連の個人消費喚起に向けた動きも追い風に、 ９月の小売売上高 （社会消費品小売総額）

  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666553.html）

(2019Q4＝100・sa)

### Page 3

3/6 
は前年同月比＋1.1％となり、前月（同
＋0.4）から加速して６ヵ月ぶりの高い
伸びとなった。 前月比も＋0.35％と前月
（同▲0.16％） から２ヵ月ぶりの拡大に
転じるとともに、 そのペースも３ヵ月ぶ
りの高い伸びとなるなど、 持ち直しの動
きが確認されている（図２） 。地域別で
は、都市部（前年比＋0.8％）の伸びが
加速しているものの、 農村 （同＋2.7％）
に比べて低い伸びにとどまるなど、 依然
として力強さを欠いている。近年のイン
ターネットの爆発的な普及を追い風に、 小売売上全体に占めるインターネットを通じたＥＣ （電子
商取引）の割合は拡大している。９月は大手ＥＣサイトの大型セールはないものの、中秋節商戦や
秋の新商品投入などを追い風に、オンライン販売は年初来前年比＋4.4％と前月（同＋4.3％）から
わずかに伸びが加速しており、小売売上全体を下支えした。店舗別では、コンビニエンスストアや
スーパーは前年を上回る伸びをみせる一方、 専門店や百貨店などは軒並み前年を下回る伸びにとど
まり、家計部門の節約志向の強まりを受けて、より低価格な業態への需要シフトが進んでいる。 
財別では、不動産に対する需要低迷が続くなかで建材（前年比▲9.2％）や家具（同▲3.0％）の
需要は引き続き低迷している。 耐久消費財の需要が弱いことに加え、 当局が実施した需要喚起策の
効果が一巡していることも重なり、自動車（前年比 ▲21.2％）は引き続き大幅に下振れする一方、
新型スマートフォンの発売などを反映して家電（前年比＋1.9％）は前年を上回る伸びで推移して
いる。しかし、雇用不安などを理由とする家計部門の節約志向が根強く残るなかで宝飾品（前年比
▲9.6％）のほか、娯楽関連（同▲1.1％）の需要も前年を下回っている。飲食料品など生活必需品
や、 日用品に対する需要には底堅い動きがみられるものの、 これはＥＣを通じた購買が常態化して
いることが影響しているとみられる。 家計部門の節約志向が一段と強まるなかでオンライン取引に
おける価格競争は激化しており、 結果的にディスインフレ圧力を脱することができない一因となっ
ている可能性がある。 このように、 個人消費を巡る動きは一見持ち直しの様相をみせているものの、
その内実については引き続き厳しい状況にあると捉えられる。
